{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016PseQaigKMk9FEB6s6enqL","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-003 RunOrchestrator — parallel eval scheduler.\n2\t\n3\tTask T03.15 / Deliverable D-0057 / Roadmap row R-057 (COMP-003).\n4\t\n5\tThe orchestrator is the single component that turns a sequence of\n6\texpanded :class:`EvalSpec` rows into a sequence of :class:`EvalOutcome`\n7\trows by scheduling per-spec :class:`EvalRunner` workers through\n8\t``concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed`` — the AC6\n9\tpattern the design-spec pins for cliEval. The implementation deliberately\n10\tmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\n11\tso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\n12\tthe same scheduling skeleton.\n13\t\n14\tResponsibilities\n15\t================\n16\t\n17\tThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\n18\tintentionally defers:\n19\t\n20\t1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n21\t   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n22\t   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n23\t   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n24\t   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n25\t   effective value.\n26\t2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n27\t   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n28\t   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n29\t   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n30\t   spec.\n31\t3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n32\t   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n33\t   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n34\t   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n35\t   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n36\t   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n37\t   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n38\t   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n39\t   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n40\t   into a status.\n41\t\n42\tThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\n43\tinstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\n44\tallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n45\t:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\n46\trunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n47\t\n48\tWorker contract\n49\t===============\n50\t\n51\tThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\n52\titself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\n53\tisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\n54\tsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\n55\tcaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\n56\tthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n57\t:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\n58\ttypically write::\n59\t\n60\t    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n61\t        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n62\t        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n63\t        return runner.run(spec)\n64\t\n65\tThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\n66\tmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\n67\ta canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n68\t\n69\tIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\n70\tof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\n71\tthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\n72\trecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n73\t:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\n74\tthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n75\t\"\"\"\n76\t\n77\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n78\t\n79\tfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\n80\tfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n81\t\n82\tfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\n83\tfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n84\t\n85\t\n86\t__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n87\t\n88\t\n89\t# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n90\t# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. Production wiring composes\n91\t# ``EvalRunner.run`` with the per-spec resource allocation the\n92\t# orchestrator deliberately does not own.\n93\tEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n94\t\n95\t\n96\tclass RunOrchestrator:\n97\t    \"\"\"Schedule per-spec eval workers in parallel via ``ThreadPoolExecutor``.\n98\t\n99\t    Parameters\n100\t    ----------\n101\t    run_one:\n102\t        Worker callable invoked once per :class:`EvalSpec`. Returns the\n103\t        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n104\t        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n105\t        out of :meth:`run`.\n106\t    cancellation_token:\n107\t        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n108\t        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). When the token\n109\t        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n110\t        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n111\t        never scheduled.\n112\t    \"\"\"\n113\t\n114\t    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n115\t    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n116\t    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n117\t\n118\t    def __init__(\n119\t        self,\n120\t        *,\n121\t        run_one: EvalWorker,\n122\t        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n123\t    ) -> None:\n124\t        if not callable(run_one):\n125\t            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n126\t        self._run_one = run_one\n127\t        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n128\t\n129\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n130\t    # Public surface\n131\t    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n132\t\n133\t    def run(\n134\t        self,\n135\t        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n136\t        *,\n137\t        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n138\t    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n139\t        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n140\t\n141\t        Parameters\n142\t        ----------\n143\t        specs:\n144\t            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n145\t            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n146\t            renders rows deterministically.\n147\t        parallel:\n148\t            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n149\t            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n150\t            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n151\t            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n152\t\n153\t        Returns\n154\t        -------\n155\t        list[EvalOutcome]\n156\t            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n157\t        \"\"\"\n158\t\n159\t        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n160\t        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n161\t        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n162\t        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n163\t        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n164\t        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n165\t        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n166\t        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n167\t            raise TypeError(\n168\t                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n169\t            )\n170\t        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n171\t            raise ValueError(\n172\t                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n173\t            )\n174\t        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n175\t\n176\t        if not specs:\n177\t            return []\n178\t\n179\t        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n180\t        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n181\t        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n182\t        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n183\t\n184\t        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n185\t        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n186\t        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n187\t        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n188\t\n189\t        with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n190\t            max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n191\t        ) as pool:\n192\t            futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n193\t\n194\t            for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n195\t                # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n196\t                # submitting new work. The implementation deliberately\nmirrors the shape used by ``src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802``\nso a reader switching between the PRD pipeline and the eval harness sees\nthe same scheduling skeleton.\n\nResponsibilities\n================\n\nThe orchestrator owns three cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer\nintentionally defers:\n\n1. **Concurrency budget.** ``max_workers`` is clamped to ``[1, 15]`` per\n   design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) with a default of ``8``. Values outside\n   the range either raise (``parallel < 1``) or saturate to the upper\n   bound (``parallel > 15``). The clamp is enforced once at the top of\n   :meth:`RunOrchestrator.run` so the rest of the body can trust the\n   effective value.\n2. **Outcome contract.** :class:`RunOrchestrator` guarantees one\n   :class:`EvalOutcome` per input :class:`EvalSpec` in the original\n   sequence order. The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) enforces the\n   N'-vs-K invariant on top of this — the orchestrator never elides a\n   spec.\n3. **Cooperative cancellation.** When a shared\n   :class:`CancellationToken` is wired (NFR-REL1 / T03.07), the\n   orchestrator stops submitting new work as soon as it observes the\n   token cancelled. Specs that were never submitted synthesise an\n   ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the orchestrator layer so the per-spec\n   contract still holds.  In-flight runners observe the same token via\n   :class:`EvalRunner` and convert worker-thread\n   ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` into ``INTERRUPTED`` outcomes\n   themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n   into a status.\n\nThe orchestrator deliberately does *not* install signal handlers. Signal\ninstallation is the CLI dispatcher's job (Phase 4): the dispatcher\nallocates a :class:`CancellationToken`, wraps the orchestrator call in a\n:class:`SignalHandlerInstaller`, and hands the token down so the eval\nrunners observe the same cancellation flag the signal handler flips.\n\nWorker contract\n===============\n\nThe orchestrator does not construct :class:`EvalRunner` instances\nitself. Building an :class:`EvalRunner` requires per-eval HOME\nisolation, scratch paths, executor wiring, and an expect-callable\nsequence — none of which the orchestrator can resolve. Instead the\ncaller supplies a ``run_one: Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]`` worker\nthat wraps everything from ``HomeIsolation`` allocation through\n:meth:`EvalRunner.run` into a single closure. Production callers will\ntypically write::\n\n    def run_one(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        home = HomeIsolation(eval_id=spec.id, ...)\n        runner = EvalRunner(home=home, ...)\n        return runner.run(spec)\n\nThis indirection keeps the orchestrator a pure scheduling primitive and\nmakes unit-testing trivial — tests substitute a stub worker that returns\na canned outcome without touching the filesystem.\n\nIf the worker raises (e.g. construction failure, unexpected propagation\nof ``KeyboardInterrupt`` because the caller declined to wire a token),\nthe orchestrator folds the exception into an ``ERRORED`` outcome\nrecording the fully-qualified exception classname — the same shape\n:func:`run_eval` (T03.04) emits when a harness step fails. This keeps\nthe per-spec contract intact even when a worker misbehaves.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom concurrent.futures import Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom typing import Callable, ClassVar, Optional, Sequence\n\nfrom .models import EvalOutcome, EvalSpec\nfrom .signal_handler import CancellationToken\n\n\n__all__ = [\"RunOrchestrator\", \"EvalWorker\"]\n\n\n# A worker callable receives an :class:`EvalSpec` and returns the\n# corresponding :class:`EvalOutcome`. Production wiring composes\n# ``EvalRunner.run`` with the per-spec resource allocation the\n# orchestrator deliberately does not own.\nEvalWorker = Callable[[EvalSpec], EvalOutcome]\n\n\nclass RunOrchestrator:\n    \"\"\"Schedule per-spec eval workers in parallel via ``ThreadPoolExecutor``.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    run_one:\n        Worker callable invoked once per :class:`EvalSpec`. Returns the\n        :class:`EvalOutcome` for that spec. Worker exceptions are folded\n        into an ``ERRORED`` outcome — the orchestrator never re-raises\n        out of :meth:`run`.\n    cancellation_token:\n        Optional :class:`CancellationToken` shared with the CLI\n        dispatcher's signal handler (NFR-REL1 / T03.07). When the token\n        is set, the orchestrator stops submitting new work and\n        synthesises an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for every spec that was\n        never scheduled.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8\n    MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1\n    MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        run_one: EvalWorker,\n        cancellation_token: Optional[CancellationToken] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        if not callable(run_one):\n            raise TypeError(\"run_one must be callable\")\n        self._run_one = run_one\n        self._cancellation_token = cancellation_token\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Public surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def run(\n        self,\n        specs: Sequence[EvalSpec],\n        *,\n        parallel: int = DEFAULT_PARALLEL,\n    ) -> list[EvalOutcome]:\n        \"\"\"Execute every spec in parallel and return one outcome per spec.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        specs:\n            Expanded :class:`EvalSpec` sequence (post FR-EXP1 expansion).\n            The returned list preserves this order so the Reporter\n            renders rows deterministically.\n        parallel:\n            Requested concurrency level. Clamped to\n            ``[MIN_PARALLEL, MAX_PARALLEL]``; values below the minimum\n            raise :class:`ValueError`, values above the maximum saturate\n            to ``MAX_PARALLEL``. Defaults to :attr:`DEFAULT_PARALLEL`.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        list[EvalOutcome]\n            One :class:`EvalOutcome` per input spec, in the input order.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        # The minimum-bound rejection is the only hard failure: a\n        # negative or zero concurrency value is almost certainly a\n        # caller bug (CLI argument parsing should have caught it\n        # earlier) and silently coercing it would mask the bug. An\n        # upper-bound value, by contrast, is the documented behaviour\n        # of design-spec §11 — \"parallel=20 clamps to 15\" — and the\n        # orchestrator must honour it without raising.\n        if not isinstance(parallel, int) or isinstance(parallel, bool):\n            raise TypeError(\n                f\"parallel must be int; got {type(parallel).__name__}\"\n            )\n        if parallel < self.MIN_PARALLEL:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"parallel must be >= {self.MIN_PARALLEL}; got {parallel}\"\n            )\n        max_workers = min(parallel, self.MAX_PARALLEL)\n\n        if not specs:\n            return []\n\n        # Pre-allocate the result slots so we can fill them by index as\n        # futures complete. ``as_completed`` yields in completion order,\n        # not submission order, so we cannot append blindly.\n        outcomes: list[Optional[EvalOutcome]] = [None] * len(specs)\n\n        # Indices for specs that were never submitted because the token\n        # was already cancelled when the scheduler reached them. They\n        # receive a synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome below.\n        cancelled_indices: list[int] = []\n\n        with ThreadPoolExecutor(\n            max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n        ) as pool:\n            futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n\n            for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n                # Cooperative cancellation: once the token flips, stop\n                # submitting new work. Already-running workers observe\n                # the token themselves (via :class:`EvalRunner`) and\n                # convert to INTERRUPTED outcomes; we synthesise an\n                # INTERRUPTED row for every spec that did not even\n                # reach the pool so the per-spec contract still holds.\n                if self._is_cancelled():\n                    cancelled_indices.append(index)\n                    continue\n                future = pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)\n                futures[future] = index\n\n            # Drain the pool. ``as_completed`` is the AC6 idiom — it\n            # yields each future the instant its worker returns,\n            # letting downstream readers (telemetry, TUI) update in\n            # near real-time without waiting for the slowest eval.\n            for future in as_completed(futures):\n                index = futures[future]\n                spec = specs[index]\n                try:\n                    outcomes[index] = future.result()\n                except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — see docstring\n                    # The worker should fold its own harness errors\n                    # into an ERRORED outcome via :func:`run_eval`. We\n                    # only get here when the worker itself raised\n                    # (construction failure, unwired-token\n                    # KeyboardInterrupt propagation, etc.). Translate\n                    # to ERRORED so :meth:`run` never re-raises.\n                    outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(spec, exc)\n\n        # Backfill INTERRUPTED outcomes for never-submitted specs after\n        # the pool has shut down so we cannot race with an in-flight\n        # cancellation.\n        for index in cancelled_indices:\n            outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n\n        # Every slot must be populated by now. The cast filters the\n        # ``Optional[...]`` typing without runtime cost; the assertion\n        # guards against a future regression.\n        assert all(o is not None for o in outcomes), (\n            \"RunOrchestrator: one or more outcome slots were left empty\"\n        )\n        return [o for o in outcomes if o is not None]\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _invoke_worker(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Indirection so subclasses / tests can hook the call site.\"\"\"\n        return self._run_one(spec)\n\n    def _is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        token = self._cancellation_token\n        return token is not None and token.is_cancelled()\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _interrupted_outcome(spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Build the synthesised ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome for a skipped spec.\n\n        Mirrors :meth:`EvalRunner._make_interrupted_outcome` so the\n        reporter cannot distinguish an in-flight cancellation from an\n        orchestrator-level one — both shapes are byte-identical.\n        \"\"\"\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"INTERRUPTED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=None,\n        )\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _errored_outcome(spec: EvalSpec, exc: BaseException) -> EvalOutcome:\n        \"\"\"Fold a worker exception into a DM-001 ``ERRORED`` outcome.\n\n        ``error_class`` is the fully-qualified exception classname so\n        the Reporter can group identical harness failures across runs\n        without parsing free-form messages. Matches the format\n        :func:`run_eval` uses for in-runner harness failures.\n        \"\"\"\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return EvalOutcome(\n            eval_id=spec.id,\n            title=spec.title,\n            status=\"ERRORED\",\n            duration_sec=0.0,\n            expects=(),\n            skip_reason=None,\n            skip_flag_triggered=None,\n            artifacts={},\n            error_class=f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\",\n        )\n","numLines":292,"startLine":1,"totalLines":292}}}

Used by\n139\t      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n140\t    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n141\t      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n142\t    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n143\t      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n144\t    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n145\t      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n146\t      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n147\t      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n148\t      default for passing results.\n149\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n150\t      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n151\t    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n152\t      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n153\t      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n154\t      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n155\t      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n156\t\n157\t    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n158\t    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n159\t    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n160\t    \"\"\"\n161\t\n162\t    name: str\n163\t    passed: bool\n164\t    message: str = \"\"\n165\t    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n166\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n167\t    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n168\t\n169\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n170\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n171\t\n172\t        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n173\t        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n174\t        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n175\t        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n176\t        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n177\t        \"\"\"\n178\t\n179\t        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n180\t        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n181\t        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n182\t        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n183\t        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n184\t        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n185\t        return payload\n186\t\n187\t\n188\t# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n189\t# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n190\t_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n191\t    \"eval_id\",\n192\t    \"expect_id\",\n193\t    \"expect_name\",\n194\t    \"expected\",\n195\t    \"actual\",\n196\t    \"message\",\n197\t    \"artifact_ref\",\n198\t    \"traceback\",\n199\t)\n200\t\n201\t\n202\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n203\tclass ExpectFailure:\n204\t    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n205\t\n206\t    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n207\t    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n208\t    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n209\t\n210\t    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n211\t      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n212\t      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n213\t    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n214\t      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n215\t      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n216\t    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n217\t      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n218\t    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n219\t      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n220\t      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n221\t    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n222\t      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n223\t    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n224\t      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n225\t      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n226\t    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n227\t      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n228\t      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n229\t      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n230\t    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n231\t      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n232\t      clean diff rather than an exception.\n233\t\n234\t    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n235\t    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n236\t    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n237\t    \"\"\"\n238\t\n239\t    eval_id: str\n240\t    expect_id: str\n241\t    expect_name: str\n242\t    expected: Any\n243\t    actual: Any\n244\t    message: str = \"\"\n245\t    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n246\t    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n247\t\n248\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n249\t        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n250\t\n251\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n252\t        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n253\t        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n254\t        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n255\t        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n256\t        \"\"\"\n257\t\n258\t        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n259\t\n260\t\n261\t# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n262\t# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n263\t_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n264\t    \"eval_id\",\n265\t    \"title\",\n266\t    \"status\",\n267\t    \"duration_sec\",\n268\t    \"expects\",\n269\t    \"skip_reason\",\n270\t    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n271\t    \"artifacts\",\n272\t    \"error_class\",\n273\t)\n274\t\n275\t\n276\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n277\tclass EvalOutcome:\n278\t    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n279\t\n280\t    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n281\t    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n282\t    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n283\t\n284\t    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n285\t      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n286\t      filenames and summary keys.\n287\t    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n288\t      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n289\t      manifest at render time.\n290\t    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n291\t      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n292\t      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n293\t      raises ``ValueError``.\n294\t    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n295\t      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n296\t    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n297\t      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n298\t      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n299\t      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n300\t    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n301\t      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n302\t      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n303\t      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n304\t      the model accepts both shapes.\n305\t    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n306\t      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n307\t      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n308\t    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n309\t      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n310\t      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n311\t      an empty mapping is the default.\n312\t    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n313\t      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n314\t      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n315\t      identical harness failures across runs.\n316\t\n317\t    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n318\t    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n319\t    \"\"\"\n320\t\n321\t    eval_id: str\n322\t    title: str\n323\t    status: EvalStatus\n324\t    duration_sec: float\n325\t    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n326\t    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n327\t    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n328\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n329\t    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n330\t\n331\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n332\t        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n333\t        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n334\t        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n335\t        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n336\t        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n337\t        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n338\t        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n339\t            raise ValueError(\n340\t                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n341\t                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n342\t            )\n343\t\n344\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n345\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n346\t\n347\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n348\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n349\t        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n350\t        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n351\t        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n352\t        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n353\t        not affect the immutable source.\n354\t        \"\"\"\n355\t\n356\t        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n357\t        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n358\t            value = getattr(self, name)\n359\t            if name == \"expects\":\n360\t                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n361\t            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n362\t                payload[name] = dict(value)\n363\t            else:\n364\t                payload[name] = value\n365\t        return payload\n366\t\n367\t\n368\t# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n369\t# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n370\t_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n371\t    \"eval_id\",\n372\t    \"outcome\",\n373\t    \"start\",\n374\t    \"end\",\n375\t    \"duration_sec\",\n376\t    \"stdout\",\n377\t    \"stderr\",\n378\t    \"artifacts\",\n379\t    \"error\",\n380\t)\n381\t\n382\t\n383\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n384\tclass EvalResult:\n385\t    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n386\t\n387\t    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n388\t    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n389\t    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n390\t    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n391\t    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n392\t    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n393\t\n394\t    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n395\t      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n396\t      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n397\t    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n398\t      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n399\t      cannot be swapped after construction.\n400\t    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n401\t      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n402\t      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n403\t      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n404\t    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n405\t      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n406\t    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n407\t      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n408\t      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n409\t      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n410\t      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n411\t      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n412\t    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n413\t      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n414\t      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n415\t    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n416\t      as ``stdout``.\n417\t    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n418\t      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n419\t      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n420\t      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n421\t      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n422\t    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n423\t      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n424\t      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n425\t      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n426\t      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n427\t\n428\t    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n429\t    stable, reviewable JSON.\n430\t    \"\"\"\n431\t\n432\t    eval_id: str\n433\t    outcome: EvalOutcome\n434\t    start: str\n435\t    end: str\n436\t    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n437\t    stdout: str = \"\"\n438\t    stderr: str = \"\"\n439\t    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n440\t    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n441\t\n442\t    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n443\t        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n444\t        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n445\t        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n446\t        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n447\t        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n448\t        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n449\t        if self.start and self.end:\n450\t            computed = (\n451\t                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n452\t            ).total_seconds()\n453\t            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n454\t            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n455\t            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n456\t\n457\t    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n458\t        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n459\t\n460\t        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n461\t        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n462\t        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n463\t        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n464\t        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n465\t        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n466\t        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n467\t        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n468\t        \"\"\"\n469\t\n470\t        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n471\t        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n472\t            value = getattr(self, name)\n473\t            if name == \"outcome\":\n474\t                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n475\t            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n476\t                payload[name] = dict(value)\n477\t            elif name == \"error\":\n478\t                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n479\t            else:\n480\t                payload[name] = value\n481\t        return payload\n482\t\n483\t\n484\tdef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n485\t    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n486\t\n487\t    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n488\t    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n489\t    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n490\t    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n491\t    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n492\t    \"\"\"\n493\t\n494\t    if error is None:\n495\t        return None\n496\t    cls = type(error)\n497\t    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n498\t    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n499\t\n500\t\n501\t# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n502\t# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n503\t# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n504\t_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n505\t    \"eval_spec\",\n506\t    \"home\",\n507\t    \"home_path\",\n508\t    \"artifacts_dir\",\n509\t    \"run_dir\",\n510\t    \"env\",\n511\t    \"stdout_path\",\n512\t    \"stderr_path\",\n513\t    \"transcript_path\",\n514\t    \"jsonl_paths\",\n515\t    \"exit_code\",\n516\t    \"stdout\",\n517\t    \"stderr\",\n518\t    \"duration_sec\",\n519\t    \"artifacts\",\n520\t)\n521\t\n522\t\n523\t@dataclass(frozen=True)\n524\tclass EvalContext:\n525\t    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n526\t\n527\t    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n528\t    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n529\t    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n530\t    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n531\t    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n532\t    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n533\t\n534\t    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n535\t    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n536\t    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n537\t\n538\t    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Stored\n539\t      by-reference; ``EvalSpec`` is itself frozen so the runner cannot\n540\t      mutate the spec after the context is built. ExpectCallables read\n541\t      ``eval_spec.id`` / ``eval_spec.expects`` / ``eval_spec.timeout_sec``\n542\t      directly off the context without having to re-thread the spec.\n543\t    * ``home``            — the per-eval ``HomeIsolation`` record\n544\t      (DM-006 / T02.04). Carries ``eval_id``, ``home_root``,\n545\t      ``session_id``, ``time_offset_sec`` and the COMP-006 method\n546\t      surface; ExpectCallables that need to call ``home.env()`` /\n547\t      ``home.state_path(...)`` (e.g. ``Expect.settings_json`` reading\n548\t      ``~/.claude/settings.json``) can do so without re-deriving the\n549\t      object.\n550\t    * ``home_path``       — the per-eval ``HOME`` directory created by\n551\t      :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Stored as a separate ``Path`` so\n552\t      ExpectCallables that only need the path avoid the\n553\t      ``home.home_path`` property indirection (and the ``RuntimeError``\n554\t      it raises when setup has not run — by the time the context is\n555\t      built the setup has already completed). The factory keeps\n556\t      ``home.home_path == home_path`` invariant.\n557\t    * ``artifacts_dir``   — directory the runner writes per-eval\n558\t      artifacts under (PTY transcripts, hook outputs, captured\n559\t      side-cars). Resolved beneath ``home_path``; primitives that emit\n560\t      diff side-cars (``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` failures) drop\n561\t      them here.\n562\t    * ``run_dir``         — the run-scoped scratch directory the\n563\t      orchestrator created (FR-G2 / T03.16). One ``run_dir`` per\n564\t      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n565\t      artifact directories live beneath it.\n566\t    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n567\t      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n568\t      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n569\t      caller overrides). ExpectCallables that need raw bytes (``Expect.stdout``)\n575\t      open this path.\n576\t    * ``stderr_path``      — same for stderr.\n577\t    * ``transcript_path``  — path to the raw PTY transcript (pre-strip,\n578\t      ANSI escapes preserved). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n579\t      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n580\t      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n581\t    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n582\t      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n583\t      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n584\t      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n585\t      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n586\t    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n587\t      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n588\t      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n589\t    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n590\t      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n591\t      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n592\t      captured.\n593\t    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n594\t      ``stdout``.\n595\t    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n596\t      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n597\t      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n598\t    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n599\t      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n600\t      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n601\t      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n602\t      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n603\t      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n604\t\n605\t    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n606\t    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n607\t    ExpectCallable can rely on.      stdout_path=stdout_path,\n702\t            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n703\t            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n704\t            jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType(dict(jsonl_paths)),\n705\t The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_spec\",\n    \"home\",\n    \"home_path\",\n    \"artifacts_dir\",\n    \"run_dir\",\n    \"env\",\n    \"stdout_path\",\n    \"stderr_path\",\n    \"transcript_path\",\n    \"jsonl_paths\",\n    \"exit_code\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalContext:\n    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n\n    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n\n    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). One ``run_dir`` per\n      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n      artifact directories live beneath it.\n    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n      caller overrides). Stored as a ``MappingProxyType`` so callers\n      cannot mutate the record post-build; the factory wraps the supplied\n      mapping in ``MappingProxyType`` to enforce this.\n    * ``stdout_path``      — filesystem path of the captured stdout\n      transcript (the PTY-stripped variant the ``PtyStream`` / T02.17\n      wrote). ExpectCallables that need raw bytes (``Expect.stdout``)\n      open this path.\n    * ``stderr_path``      — same for stderr.\n    * ``transcript_path``  — path to the raw PTY transcript (pre-strip,\n      ANSI escapes preserved). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n      captured.\n    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n      ``stdout``.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n\n    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n    ExpectCallable can rely on. Mapping fields are additionally wrapped\n    in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` to prevent the more subtle attack\n    of mutating ``ctx.env[\"HOME\"]`` (frozen-dataclass blocks\n    ``__setattr__``, not method calls on field values).\n\n    Use :meth:`from_runner_state` to construct an ``EvalContext`` from\n    the runner's internal state; direct ``EvalContext(...)`` construction\n    is permitted (tests build minimal contexts for ExpectCallable unit\n    tests) but the factory is the single place runner-internal mappings\n    are wrapped in ``MappingProxyType`` and the ``home.home_path ==\n    home_path`` invariant is enforced.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\"\n    home: \"HomeIsolation\"\n    home_path: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n    run_dir: Path\n    env: Mapping[str, str]\n    stdout_path: Path\n    stderr_path: Path\n    transcript_path: Path\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path]\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str]\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Wrap mapping fields in MappingProxyType so callers that share\n        # the context across ExpectCallables cannot mutate the underlying\n        # mapping. This makes it safe to\n26\tshare across threads inside the parallel orchestrator (R-058 / T03.16)\n27\twithout locking. Equality is structural across all four fields via the\n28\tdataclass-generated ``__eq__``.\n29\t\n30\t1. ``eval_id``         — the FR-SCH2-validated eval identifier. They mutate process-local filesystem state (and an internal\n56\tprivate slot tracking the created HOME path) without ever mutating the\n57\tfour DM-006 fields:\n58\t\n59\t* :meth:`setup`                 — ``mkdtemp`` under ``home_root`` and stash\n60\t  the created path on a private ``_home_path`` slot via\n61\t  ``object.__setattr__``. The re-check closes that\n238\t       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n239\t    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n240\t       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n241\t       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n242\t       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n243\t       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n244\t       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n245\t       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n246\t       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n247\t       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n248\t       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n249\t       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n250\t       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n251\t    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n252\t       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n253\t       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n254\t       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n255\t       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n256\t       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n257\t       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n258\t       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n259\t       check would have passed.\n260\t\n261\t    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n262\t    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n263\t    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n264\t    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n265\t    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n266\t\n267\t    Args:\n268\t        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n269\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Its\n280\t            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n281\t            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n282\t            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n283\t            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n284\t            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n285\t            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n286\t            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n287\t\n288\t    Raises:\n289\t        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. Implementations should raise on failure;\n    ``run_eval`` classifies any non-``KeyboardInterrupt`` /\n    ``SystemExit`` exception as ``ERRORED``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> ObservedRun:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal sentinel for \"no observed run yet\"\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_NO_OBSERVED_RUN = ObservedRun(\n    exit_code=-1,\n    stdout=\"\",\n    stderr=\"\",\n    duration_sec=0.0,\n    jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType({}),\n    artifacts=MappingProxyType({}),\n)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Public entrypoint\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef run_eval(\n    spec: EvalSpec,\n    *,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    run_dir: Path,\n    artifacts_dir: Path,\n    stdout_path: Path,\n    stderr_path: Path,\n    transcript_path: Path,\n    executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n    expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n    deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]] = None,\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Execute the FR-LC1 7-step lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The function is the *skeleton* on top of which COMP-004 EvalRunner\n    (T03.05) layers per-eval JSONL logging and per-eval timeout\n    enforcement. See ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` for the full lifecycle\n    + status-mapping contract.\n\n    Arguments are keyword-only (after ``spec``) so future field\n    additions cannot silently re-bind positional callers. The default\n    ``deploy_hooks`` resolves to the COMP-014 ``deploy_hooks_to``\n    function; tests inject a stub.\n\n    Raises\n    ------\n    KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit\n        Re-raised verbatim after running ``home.teardown(keep=True)`` so\n        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) can convert them into an ``INTERRUPTED``\n        outcome at the orchestrator layer. Every other exception is\n        captured and folded into an ``EvalOutcome`` with status\n        ``ERRORED``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Per-step exception capture lives in ``_LifecycleState`` so the\n    # status-mapping helper can read every step's result without\n    # threading positional state through the function body.\n    state = _LifecycleState(spec=spec)\n\n    try:\n        # Step 1 — build isolation. ``HomeIsolation.setup`` is itself\n        # idempotency-guarded; the atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) preserves\n        # the partial HOME on failure so ``teardown(keep=True)`` keeps\n        # forensic artifacts on disk.\n        try:\n            home.setup(config=config)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"setup\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        home_path = home.home_path\n\n        # Step 2 — deploy hooks. Injectable so tests stub the deploy\n        # without a real ``hooks.json`` on disk.\n        try:\n            deploy_hooks(home_path)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"deploy_hooks\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        # Step 5 — observe.\n        try:\n            observed = executor.observe(exec_ctx)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"observe\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n        state.record_observed(observed)\n\n        # Step 6 — assert. Build the per-Expect EvalContext now that we\n        # have stdout/stderr/exit_code/duration, then apply each\n        # callable in declaration order. A callable that raises folds\n        # into ERRORED with a partial ``expects`` tuple (whatever\n        # callables ran before the raise).\n        ctx = EvalContext.from_runner_state(\n            eval_spec=spec,\n            home=home,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=observed.jsonl_paths,\n            env=exec_ctx.env,\n            exit_code=observed.exit_code,\n            stdout=observed.stdout,\n            stderr=observed.stderr,\n            duration_sec=observed.duration_sec,\n            artifacts=observed.artifacts,\n        )\n\n        for callable_ in expect_callables:\n            try:\n                result = callable_(ctx)\n            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n                raise\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n                state.record_harness_failure(step=\"assert\", exc=exc)\n                return _finalize(\n                    state=state,\n                    home=home,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            state.record_expect(result)\n\n        return _finalize(\n            state=state,\n            home=home,\n            on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n            keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n        )\n\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        # NFR-REL1 contract: cooperative cancellation. Teardown the\n        # partial HOME with ``keep=True`` so forensics survive, then\n        # re-raise so the orchestrator's signal handler can convert this\n        # into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome record_observed(self, observed: ObservedRun) -> None:\n        self.observed = observed\n\n    def record_expect(self, result: ExpectResult) -> None:\n        self.expects.append(result)\n\n    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n        self.harness_failure_step = step\n        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n\n\ndef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. Goal\n9\t\n10\tSchedule per-spec `EvalRunner` workers in parallel via\n11\t`concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor + as_completed` — the AC6\n12\tpattern the cliEval design-spec pins (referenced shape:\n13\t`src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`). The orchestrator owns\n14\tthree cross-cutting concerns the lifecycle layer (T03.04) and runner\n15\tclass (T03.05) deliberately deferred:\n16\t\n17\t1. **Concurrency budget** — `max_workers` clamped to `[1, 15]` per\n18\t   design-spec §11 / NFR-PERF2 with default `8`.\n19\t2. **Outcome contract** — one `EvalOutcome` per input `EvalSpec`, in\n20\t   input order, regardless of completion order (the Reporter / FR-RPT1\n21\t   N'-vs-K invariant relies on this).\n22\t3. **Cooperative cancellation** — when the shared `CancellationToken`\n23\t   (NFR-REL1 / T03.07) flips, scheduling stops and unsubmitted specs\n24\t   synthesise an `INTERRUPTED` outcome so the per-spec contract still\n25\t   holds.\n26\t\n27\tThe orchestrator does **not** install signal handlers. Keeping the orchestrator agnostic of construction\n49\tdetails lets the CLI dispatcher own resource allocation and lets tests\n50\tsubstitute a stub worker that returns a canned outcome.\n51\t\n52\t### 2.2 `RunOrchestrator`\n53\t\n54\t| Attribute / method | Contract |\n55\t|---|---|\n56\t| `DEFAULT_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 8` | Default concurrency level. |\n57\t| `MIN_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 1` | Minimum accepted `parallel`. Values below raise `ValueError`. |\n58\t| `MAX_PARALLEL: ClassVar[int] = 15` | Upper bound; values above saturate. |\n59\t| `__init__(*, run_one, cancellation_token=None)` | Worker callable + optional token. Non-callable `run_one` raises `TypeError`. |\n60\t| `run(specs, *, parallel=DEFAULT_PARALLEL) -> list[EvalOutcome]` | Schedule and collect outcomes. Empty `specs` → `[]`. |\n61\t\n62\t### 2.3 Outcome shape\n63\t\n64\tThe orchestrator emits two synthesised outcome shapes itself:\n65\t\n66\t| Shape | Trigger | Status | `duration_sec` | `error_class` |\n67\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n68\t| Interrupted | Cancellation token set before spec submitted | `INTERRUPTED` | `0.0` | `None` |\n69\t| Errored | Worker callable raised | `ERRORED` | `0.0` | `f\"{type(exc).__module__}.{type(exc).__qualname__}\"` |\n70\t\n71\tEvery other outcome shape is returned verbatim from the worker —\n72\tnotably `PASS` / `FAIL` / `TIMEOUT` / `INTERRUPTED` from the in-runner\n73\tclassification (D-0049 / D-0050).\n74\t\n75\t## 3. Scheduling Pattern\n76\t\n77\t```python\n78\twith ThreadPoolExecutor(\n79\t    max_workers=max_workers, thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"\n80\t) as pool:\n81\t    futures: dict[Future[EvalOutcome], int] = {}\n82\t    for index, spec in enumerate(specs):\n83\t        if self._is_cancelled():\n84\t            cancelled_indices.append(index)\n85\t            continue\n86\t        futures[pool.submit(self._invoke_worker, spec)] = index\n87\t\n88\t    for future in as_completed(futures):\n89\t        index = futures[future]\n90\t        try:\n91\t            outcomes[index] = future.result()\n92\t        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n93\t            outcomes[index] = self._errored_outcome(specs[index], exc)\n94\t\n95\tfor index in cancelled_indices:\n96\t    outcomes[index] = self._interrupted_outcome(specs[index])\n97\t```\n98\t\n99\tNotes:\n100\t\n101\t* `outcomes` is pre-allocated and indexed by submission order so the\n102\t  return-value order is independent of completion order.\n103\t* The cancellation check is per-spec inside the submission loop, not\n104\t  per-future. In-flight runners observe the same token via\n105\t  `EvalRunner._cancellation_token` (D-0050) and convert worker-thread\n106\t  `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` into `INTERRUPTED` outcomes\n107\t  themselves — the orchestrator never has to translate an exception\n108\t  into a status.\n109\t* `thread_name_prefix=\"cliEval\"` makes per-eval threads identifiable in\n110\t  `py-spy` / `ps` output for diagnosing hangs.\n111\t\n112\t## 4. Parallelism Validation\n113\t\n114\t`tests/cli/eval/test_orchestrator.py::test_three_eval_suite_runs_faster_than_3x_sequential`\n115\tasserts the runtime contract directly:\n116\t\n117\t* 3 specs × 0.2s sleep each.\n118\t* `parallel=3`.\n119\t* Elapsed must be `< 3 × eval_duration_sec` (i.e. `< 0.6s`). A truly\n120\t  serialised execution would be ≥ 0.6s. The 2× margin absorbs\n121\t  ThreadPoolExecutor startup overhead on slow CI hosts.\n122\t\n123\tConcurrency-bound tests\n124\t(`test_parallel_above_max_clamps_to_fifteen`,\n125\t`test_parallel_one_serialises`) snapshot the maximum number of workers\n126\trunning simultaneously to confirm the clamp is honoured at runtime, not\n127\tjust at validation time.\n128\t\n129\t## 5. Out of Scope\n130\t\n131\t* **Signal handler installation.** Phase 4 CLI dispatcher owns this.\n132\t* **Partial summary writing.** Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) reads the\n133\t  outcomes the orchestrator returns and persists `summary.{md,json}`.\n134\t* **Exit-code translation.** Phase 4 dispatcher maps the outcome set\n135\t  to `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` (`3`), `1`, `2`, or `0` per design-spec §4.\n136\t* **Per-eval timeout enforcement.** Already in `EvalRunner` (D-0049 +\n137\t  D-0050); the orchestrator never time-slices a single eval.\n138\t* **15-eval isolation integration scenario.** T03.16 owns the end-to-end\n139\t  proof; this deliverable owns only the scheduling primitive.\n140\t\n141\t## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n142\t\n143\t| Mode | Behaviour |\n144\t|---|---|\n145\t| `parallel < 1` | `ValueError` raised before any submission. |\n146\t| `parallel` not `int` (or `bool`) | `TypeError` raised. |\n147\t| `parallel > 15` | Saturates to `MAX_PARALLEL = 15`. |\n148\t| Worker raises | Folded into `ERRORED` outcome with `error_class`. `run()` never re-raises. |\n149\t| Token pre-cancelled | No worker is submitted; every spec receives an `INTERRUPTED` outcome. |\n150\t| Token cancelled mid-run | Specs already submitted run to completion (their in-runner observation of the same token converts them to `INTERRUPTED` if they have not finished); unsubmitted specs get synthesised `INTERRUPTED`. |\n151\t| `run_one` not callable at construction | `TypeError` from `__init__`. |\n152\t| Empty `specs` | Returns `[]` immediately; no pool started. |\n153\t\n154\t## 7. Out of Scope\n\n* **Signal handler installation.** Phase 4 CLI dispatcher owns this.\n* **Partial summary writing.** Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) reads the\n  outcomes the orchestrator returns and persists `summary.{md,json}`.\n* **Exit-code translation.** Phase 4 dispatcher maps the outcome set\n  to `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` (`3`), `1`, `2`, or `0` per design-spec §4.\n* **Per-eval timeout enforcement.** Already in `EvalRunner` (D-0049 +\n  D-0050); the orchestrator never time-slices a single eval.\n* **15-eval isolation integration scenario.** T03.16 owns the end-to-end\n  proof; this deliverable owns only the scheduling primitive.\n\n## 6. Failure Modes & Containment\n\n| Mode | Behaviour |\n|---|---|\n| `parallel < 1` | `ValueError` raised before any submission. |\n| `parallel` not `int` (or `bool`) | `TypeError` raised. |\n| `parallel > 15` | Saturates to `MAX_PARALLEL = 15`. |\n| Worker raises | Folded into `ERRORED` outcome with `error_class`. `run()` never re-raises. |\n| Token pre-cancelled | No worker is submitted; every spec receives an `INTERRUPTED` outcome. |\n| Token cancelled mid-run | Specs already submitted run to completion (their in-runner observation of the same token converts them to `INTERRUPTED` if they have not finished); unsubmitted specs get synthesised `INTERRUPTED`. |\n| `run_one` not callable at construction | `TypeError` from `__init__`. |\n| Empty `specs` | Returns `[]` immediately; no pool started. |\n\n## 7. Acceptance Criteria → Test Mapping\n\n| AC | Test |\n|---|---|\n| One `EvalOutcome` per expanded spec, input order preserved. | `TestOneOutcomePerSpec::test_outcome_order_matches_input_order`, `TestOneOutcomePerSpec::test_every_spec_gets_exactly_one_outcome` |\n| `parallel=20` clamps to 15. | `TestParallelClamp::test_parallel_above_max_clamps_to_fifteen` |\n| `parallel < 1` rejected. | `TestParallelClamp::test_zero_parallel_rejected`, `TestParallelClamp::test_negative_parallel_rejected` |\n| 3-eval suite runs faster than 3× slowest-eval duration. | `test_three_eval_suite_runs_faster_than_3x_sequential` |\n| Pre-cancelled token → all `INTERRUPTED`, no worker invoked. | `TestCancellation::test_pre_cancelled_token_skips_all_specs` |\n| Mid-run cancel stops new submissions. | `TestCancellation::test_mid_run_cancel_stops_new_submissions` |\n| Worker exception folded into `ERRORED` outcome. | `TestWorkerExceptionFolding::test_runtime_error_from_worker_folds_to_errored` |\n| Constructor rejects non-callable worker. | `TestConstructorGuards::test_non_callable_worker_rejected` |\n\nSee `evidence.md` for the recorded pytest output.\n\n## 8. References\n\n* Reference pattern: `src/superclaude/cli/prd/executor.py:774-802`\n  (PRD pipeline's parallel step scheduler).\n* Upstream contracts: D-0049 (EvalRunner class), D-0050 (signal\n  handling + cancellation token).\n* Downstream consumers: T03.16 (FR-G2 15-eval integration), Phase 4\n  CLI dispatcher (signal handler installation + exit-code translation),\n  COMP-008 Reporter (FR-RPT1 N'-vs-K invariant on the returned outcome\n  list).\n","numLines":179,"startLine":1,"totalLines":179}}}

The CLI/reporter layer downstream sees a\n70\tclean per-spec list and can render the error class in the report.\n71\t\n72\tA separate question is whether `KeyboardInterrupt`/`SystemExit` should fold or\n73\tpropagate.
